log is created with the process default permissions\n\n- **Severity:** Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-732: Incorrect Permission Assignment for Critical Resource\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:376-381` (`FileEventLogger` constructor)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:376-381\n            try {\n                if (file.getParent() != null) {\n                    Files.createDirectories(file.getParent());\n                }\n                this.writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                    StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** The audit file is the most sensitive artifact this component produces: one line per authentication attempt with the username, the source address, the outcome, the failure count and the lock state. It is created by `Files.newBufferedWriter` with no `FileAttribute`, so it inherits the process umask — on essentially every Linux deployment that is `0644`, readable by every local account — and `Files.createDirectories` likewise creates any missing parent directory world-readable/executable. Any low-privileged principal on the host (another tenant on a shared machine, a limited service account, a web-shell) can then read the whole trail, which tells them exactly which accounts exist, which are disabled, which are already locked, and which source addresses are being used by legitimate users — a curated target list for spraying that costs the attacker nothing and skips every locked account. Read access is also the less interesting half: if the containing directory is group- or world-writable (common for `/tmp`-adjacent defaults, and `auth-demo.log` is relative to the launch cwd), the same principal can append forged `LOGIN_SUCCESS` lines or truncate the file, so the trail cannot be relied on as evidence for the disciplinary or legal purposes log rows are explicitly documented for. The code protects passwords carefully (`toString` redaction, buffer clearing) while leaving the record of every login world-readable.\n\n**Fix:** Request the mode at creation time so there is no window, and correct an inherited file that already exists (the `CREATE`+`APPEND` combination is precisely the case where a wrong-mode file pre-exists). On non-POSIX filesystems the attribute is silently ignored, so apply the platform equivalent.\n\n```java\n// corrected\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n        PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\");\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY_DIR =\n        PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rwx------\");\n\n        public FileEventLogger(Path file, PrintStream echo) {\n            if (file == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"file is required\");\n            }\n            this.file = file;\n            this.echo = echo;\n            try {\n                if (file.getParent() != null) {\n                    Files.createDirectories(file.getParent(),\n                        PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY_DIR));\n                }\n                this.writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                    PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(OWNER_ONLY),\n                    StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n                if (!Files.isSymbolicLink(file)) {\n                    Files.setPosixFilePermissions(file, OWNER_ONLY);   // pre-existing inherited file\n                }\n            } catch (IOException cannotOpen) {\n                throw new UncheckedIOException(\"cannot open audit log \" + file, cannotOpen);\n            }\n        }\n```\nRequires `java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission(s)` and `java.util.Set`; on Windows set an ACL (`icacls <file> /inheritance:r /grant:r \"%USERNAME%:F\"`) or reject the filesystem, and put the log on a volume where only the service account and the log shipper have access.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-05: A failed audit write aborts the login with an unchecked exception\n\n- **Severity:** Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-754: Improper Check or Handling of Exceptional Conditions (unchecked exception escaping a request path — CWE-248)\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:388-402` (`FileEventLogger.log`), reached from `UserAuthentication.java:482-483`, `518-521`, `527-532`\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:388-402\n        public void log(Event event) {\n            String line = event.format();\n            synchronized (lock) {\n                try {\n                    writer.write(line);\n                    writer.newLine();\n                    writer.flush();\n                } catch (IOException writeFailed) {\n                    throw new UncheckedIOException(\"cannot write audit log \" + file, writeFailed);\n                }\n            }\n            if (echo != null) {\n                echo.println(line);\n            }\n        }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** Every `authenticate()` call writes at least one audit record *before returning*, and a write failure is converted into an unchecked exception that escapes the authentication API. Nobody in this component catches it — `main` only wraps CLI argument handling — so a full disk, a quota, an immutable attribute, a logrotate that unlinked the file, or a hard-mounted NFS server that stopped responding turns every subsequent login into an exception thrown at the caller instead of a `Result`: a logging outage becomes an authentication outage, with a stack trace as the user-facing answer. The failure mode is also unrecordable — the security event that matters most (\"the audit trail stopped writing\") is exactly the one that cannot be written, and no operator is alerted, so an attacker who fills the disk or otherwise breaks the log gets a window in which their activity is not recorded while the service keeps throwing errors that look like infrastructure noise. Two aggravating details: the throw happens while `synchronized (lock)` is held, so all other login threads block behind the failing writer (a stalled device turns into a whole-service stall, a CWE-400 amplifier), and the audit record is written *after* the decision, so a mid-flow throw can leave state already mutated (`recordFailure` at `523`, `recordSuccess` at `526`) with no event describing it.\n\n**Fix:** Never let the audit sink throw into the request path; report the outcome and let the caller apply an explicit policy — fail closed on the records that assert success, keep the trail's failure visible to the operator, and take the disk write off the lock.\n\n```java\n// corrected: the sink reports, it does not punish the caller\n        /** @return true when the record reached the file; never throws to the caller. */\n        public boolean log(Event event) {\n            String line = event.format();\n            boolean written;\n            synchronized (lock) {\n                try {\n                    writer.write(line);\n                    writer.newLine();\n                    writer.flush();\n                    written = true;\n                } catch (IOException | RuntimeException writeFailed) {\n                    written = false;\n                    lastWriteFailure = writeFailed;                       // read by the health check\n                    System.err.println(\"AUDIT DOWN: cannot write \" + file + \": \" + writeFailed);\n                }\n            }\n            if (written && echo != null) {\n                echo.println(line);\n            }\n            return written;\n        }\n```\n```java\n// corrected: authenticate() decides what an unwritable record means\n        long millis = elapsedMillis(startedAt);\n        if (!success) {\n            log.log(new Event(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, username, remoteAddress, reason,\n                null, attempts, 0, \"refused\"));\n            return new Result(Outcome.FAILURE, reason, username, millis, attempts);\n        }\n        if (!log.log(new Event(EventType.LOGIN_SUCCESS, username, remoteAddress, Reason.OK,\n            null, attempts, cleared, \"ok\"))) {\n            // No durable record: do not certify a success we cannot evidence.\n            return new Result(Outcome.ERROR, Reason.INTERNAL_ERROR, username, millis, attempts);\n        }\n        return new Result(Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.OK, username, millis, attempts);\n```\nAdd an alerting hook on `lastWriteFailure` (or a health-check endpoint), keep an in-memory ring buffer of unsent events for replay after the disk recovers, and consider an async writer so the flush is not on the authentication critical path.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-06: `register()` and `accountExists()` are a live account-enumeration oracle\n\n- **Severity:** Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-204: Observable Response Discrepancy in Authentication Response (also CWE-203)\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:439-454` (`register`), `UserAuthentication.java:559-562` (`accountExists`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:439-454\n    public boolean register(String rawUsername, char[] password) {\n        String username = normalizeUsername(rawUsername);\n        if (username == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"username must be \" + USERNAME_MIN + \"-\"\n                + USERNAME_MAX + \" characters of [A-Za-z0-9._-] after trimming\");\n        }\n        if (!isHashablePassword(password)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password is empty, too long, ends with NUL or \"\n                + \"contains an unpaired surrogate\");\n        }\n        PasswordHash hash = PasswordHash.generate(password, config.iterations, random);\n        boolean created = accounts.register(username, hash);\n        log.log(new Event(EventType.ACCOUNT_REGISTERED, username, null, Reason.OK, null, 0, 0,\n            created ? \"created\" : \"duplicate\"));\n        return created;\n    }\n\n// UserAuthentication.java:559-562\n    public boolean accountExists(String username) {\n        String name = normalizeUsername(username);\n        return name != null && accounts.find(name) != null;\n    }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** A lot of effort went into making `authenticate()` non-informative: identical user-facing wording for every refusal (`231`-`243`), internal-only reasons, and an equal-work derivation so that an unknown username costs the same as a known one (`474`-`478`). `register()` and `accountExists()` return exactly what that hardening exists to withhold. `register()` gives the caller `true`/`false` for \"was free\" / \"already taken\" — the standard signup-form enumeration primitive, and any UI that shows \"that username is unavailable\" leaks the whole account list one request at a time; the audit `detail` of `created`/`duplicate` (`452`) is the same oracle for anyone who can read the trail. `accountExists()` removes even that pretence: a normalized map lookup, no password required, no KDF cost, no failure counter, no lockout, and no rate limit — an attacker can enumerate millions of candidate names in seconds, at zero CPU cost to themselves, and hand the confirmed subset to the spray described in SEC-07. Note the inconsistency within the class itself: `setEnabled` (`534`-`543`) and `unlock` (`546`-`556`) were deliberately written to return a generic `Outcome` rather than existence information, so this is the remaining gap rather than a different design choice.\n\n**Fix:** Remove the existence oracle from the public surface and make registration outcome-blind to the caller. Keep the truth in the audit trail, where responders need it, not in the API, where callers do not.\n\n```java\n// corrected: registration cannot be used as a probe\n    public Result register(String rawUsername, char[] password, String remoteAddress) {\n        String username = normalizeUsername(rawUsername);\n        if (username == null || !isHashablePassword(password)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"registration request rejected\");\n        }\n        PasswordHash hash = PasswordHash.generate(password, config.iterations, random);\n        boolean created = accounts.register(username, hash);\n        // The caller sees one message either way; only the audit trail distinguishes them.\n        log.log(new Event(EventType.ACCOUNT_REGISTERED, username, remoteAddress, Reason.OK,\n            null, 0, 0, created ? \"created\" : \"duplicate\"));\n        return new Result(Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.OK, username, 0L, 0);\n    }\n\n    // accountExists() is deleted. An administrative feature that genuinely needs it queries\n    // the AccountStore behind its own authorization check, outside this class.\n```\nWhere a signup flow must tell a human \"taken\", use a rate-limited, CAPTCHA-or-authenticated endpoint outside the authentication path, and never a method reachable from the login object.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-07: The only attempt limit is per-account, enabling both spraying and targeted lockout denial\n\n- **Severity:** Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:465-533` (`authenticate`), `UserAuthentication.java:892-904` (`Account.recordFailure`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:892-904\n        synchronized FailureState recordFailure(Instant now, Config config) {\n            if (lastFailure != null\n                && Duration.between(lastFailure, now).compareTo(config.failureWindow) > 0) {\n                failures = 0;\n            }\n            failures++;\n            lastFailure = now;\n            boolean tripped = failures >= config.maxFailures;\n            if (tripped) {\n                lockedUntil = now.plus(config.lockDuration);\n            }\n            return new FailureState(failures, tripped);\n        }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** The counter lives inside a single `Account`, so `maxFailures` bounds attempts *per username*, never per source, per network or globally — and `remoteAddress` is sanitized and logged but never used in any decision. That yields both classic failures of CWE-307. First, password spraying is essentially free: one source tries `hunter2`, `hunter2!`, `hunter21234` against `alice`, then moves on; each account absorbs at most `maxFailures` guesses before it locks, so 10 000 usernames yield 30 000 guesses per failure window with no throttle ever engaging, and every account that does lock is a side benefit. Second, the lockout is a denial-of-access weapon in the attacker's hands: the threshold is reachable by anyone who can name a username (and SEC-06 tells them which names are valid), so an adversary can lock out a specific victim — or the entire user base — before launching the actual guess against an account nobody can unlock without the operator call at `546`-`556`, which is itself unauthenticated. The cost ordering makes it worse: `authenticate` derives the PBKDF2 key at `477`, before the enabled check (`496`), the lockout check (`502`-`506`) and the comparison, so every unauthenticated request is paid for at the full iteration count by the server — a spray is simultaneously a CPU exhaustion attack (SEC-01 removes the ceiling on that cost entirely). `README.md` names rate limiting as a complementary control, but the component exposes no hook through which one could be applied.\n\n**Fix:** Budget by source *before* spending any KDF work, and stop letting a per-account counter be a denial primitive: escalate delay instead of hard-locking, and cap per-source attempts so the attacker pays rather than the victim.\n\n```java\n// corrected: cheap source budget consulted before any derivation\n    private final Map<String, SlidingWindow> budgetBySource = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n\n    private boolean sourceAllowed(String remoteAddress, Instant now) {\n        if (remoteAddress == null || remoteAddress.isEmpty()) {\n            return true;                              // unattributable: log loudly, still budget globally\n        }\n        return budgetBySource.computeIfAbsent(remoteAddress,\n                key -> new SlidingWindow(config.maxAttemptsPerSource, config.sourceWindow))\n            .tryAcquire(now);\n    }\n\n    public Result authenticate(String rawUsername, char[] password, String remoteAddress) {\n        Instant startedAt = config.clock.instant();\n        String username = normalizeUsername(rawUsername);\n        if (username == null || !isHashablePassword(password)) {\n            return refuse(username, Reason.INVALID_INPUT, remoteAddress, startedAt, 0, 0);\n        }\n        if (!sourceAllowed(remoteAddress, startedAt)) {\n            // Counted and refused without deriving anything: the request cannot buy CPU.\n            return refuse(username, Reason.RATE_LIMITED, remoteAddress, startedAt, 0, 0);\n        }\n        // ... unchanged: equal-work digest, enabled check, lock check, constant-time compare ...\n    }\n```\n```java\n// corrected: escalating delay instead of a free lockout\n        synchronized FailureState recordFailure(Instant now, Config config) {\n            if (lastFailure != null\n                && Duration.between(lastFailure, now).compareTo(config.failureWindow) > 0) {\n                failures = 0;\n            }\n            failures++;\n            lastFailure = now;\n            if (failures >= config.maxFailures) {\n                long seconds = config.lockDuration.toSeconds()\n                    << Math.min(failures - config.maxFailures, MAX_BACKOFF_STEPS);\n                lockedUntil = now.plus(Duration.ofSeconds(Math.min(seconds, MAX_LOCK_SECONDS)));\n            }\n            return new FailureState(failures, failures >= config.maxFailures);\n        }\n```\nAdd `RATE_LIMITED` to `Reason` with its own `userMessage()` entry (the `default` branch currently answers \"Invalid username or password.\" for any new reason), add `maxAttemptsPerSource`/`sourceWindow` to `Config`, and require an authenticated administrative identity for `unlock` and `setEnabled`.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-08: Working credentials are hardcoded in `main` and printed on every run\n\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials (with CWE-522 exposure through stdout)\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:958-965` (`main`), `AuthTest.java:42`\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:958-965\n            auth.register(\"alice\", \"winter-Frost-42\".toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"bob\", \"correct horse battery staple\".toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"dave\", \"hunter2-hunter2\".toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"erin\", \"sup3r-Secret!\".toCharArray());\n            auth.setEnabled(\"erin\", false);\n            out.println();\n            out.println(\"Demo accounts: alice / winter-Frost-42, bob / correct horse battery staple\");\n            out.println(\"Accounts dave (lockout demo) and erin (disabled) also exist.\");\n\n// AuthTest.java:42\n    private static final char[] PW = \"winter-Frost-42\".toCharArray();\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** Four real, credential-shaped secrets are compiled into the artifact, and two of them are echoed to stdout on every single run. Three concrete consequences. (1) These strings are the component's reference usage: they appear in the banner, in the test suite as `PW`, and therefore in any README, blog post, wrapper script or copy-pasted deployment that grows out of the demo — the classic path by which a \"demo password\" ends up in production, where CWE-798 means anyone with the jar also has the account. (2) Printing them puts credentials into terminal scrollback, CI job logs, container stdout collectors and support screenshots — the same channels that the careful `toString` redaction (`246`-`251`, `774`-`777`) and the field sanitizer exist to keep secrets out of. (3) `hunter2-hunter2` is a joke password and a guaranteed hit for any spray tool's default doubling rule, and `erin`/`sup3r-Secret!` shows a disabled account with a password that is guessable by pattern — the demo teaches exactly the password choices the rest of the design has to defend against.\n\n**Fix:** Never put a usable secret in source, and never print one either. Mint the demo secrets at run time from a non-source location, or prompt for them.\n\n```java\n// corrected\n            SecureRandom rng = new SecureRandom();\n            auth.register(\"alice\", randomDemoSecret(rng).toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"bob\", randomDemoSecret(rng).toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"dave\", randomDemoSecret(rng).toCharArray());\n            auth.register(\"erin\", randomDemoSecret(rng).toCharArray());\n            auth.setEnabled(\"erin\", false);\n            out.println();\n            out.println(\"Demo accounts alice/bob/dave were created with one-time passwords,\");\n            out.println(\"which are not printed. dave demonstrates lockout, erin is disabled.\");\n            out.println(\"Run with --interactive to log in as a username you choose.\");\n\n    private static String randomDemoSecret(SecureRandom rng) {\n        final String ALPHABET =\n            \"abcdefghijkmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789!@#$%^&*-_=+\";\n        StringBuilder secret = new StringBuilder(20);\n        for (int i = 0; i < 20; i++) {\n            secret.append(ALPHABET.charAt(rng.nextInt(ALPHABET.length())));\n        }\n        return secret.toString();\n    }\n```\nIf a caller must supply the secret, read it from an environment variable or `console.readPassword()` rather than embedding or echoing it; keep the literal only inside `AuthTest.java`, where it can never be mistaken for a deployable credential, and add a CI grep that fails on credential-shaped literals in `main`.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-09: `--log` is honoured verbatim, making the audit destination an arbitrary-file write\n\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-22: Improper Limitation of a Pathname to a Restricted Directory ('Path Traversal')\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:950` (call site), `UserAuthentication.java:1065-1067` (`pathArgument`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:950\n            Path logFile = pathArgument(args, \"--log\", \"auth-demo.log\");\n\n// UserAuthentication.java:1065-1067\n    private static Path pathArgument(String[] args, String name, String fallback) {\n        return Path.of(argument(args, name, fallback));\n    }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** The audit destination comes straight from argv and is turned into a path with no validation at all — no `normalize()`, no rejection of absolute paths or `..` segments, no containment inside an audit directory. `FileEventLogger` then *creates* the missing parent directories (`377`-`379`) and opens the target with `CREATE, WRITE, APPEND` (`380`-`381`), which is an arbitrary-file-creation-and-append primitive anywhere the process user can write. The content is line-sanitized, but the sanitization does not matter here: what matters is that an attacker-controlled *target* receives attacker-influenced `key=value` lines. Anything that lets an untrusted party affect this process's arguments — a CI parameter, a job or unit-file template, a config-management field, a wrapper script that interpolates user input into the command line — turns `--log ../../../../home/victim/.ssh/environment`, `--log /etc/cron.d/x` (if privileged), or `--log ./app/config overrides.yml` into a write primitive, plus directory creation at a chosen location. The asymmetry inside `main` is telling: `--iterations` is validated by the constructor it flows into, `--log` is trusted outright, and `--log` is the flag that decides where the security record goes.\n\n**Fix:** Accept a file *name*, resolve it against a configured audit directory, and refuse anything that resolves outside it.\n\n```java\n// corrected\n    private static Path pathArgument(String[] args, String name, String fallback) {\n        Path base = Path.of(System.getProperty(\"auth.audit.dir\", \".\")).toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        Path requested = Path.of(argument(args, name, fallback));\n        if (requested.isAbsolute() || requested.getNameCount() != 1) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(name + \" must be a bare file name inside \" + base);\n        }\n        Path resolved = base.resolve(requested).normalize();\n        if (!resolved.startsWith(base) || !base.equals(resolved.getParent())\n            || Files.isSymbolicLink(resolved)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(name + \" resolves outside the audit directory\");\n        }\n        return resolved;\n    }\n```\nFor a real deployment, do not take the destination from argv at all: resolve it from trusted server configuration, create the directory once at provisioning time with owner-only mode (SEC-04), and let the process append only inside it.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-10: The KDF cost policy is a 1 000-iteration floor, two orders of magnitude below the project's own default\n\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-916: Use of Password Hash With Insufficient Computational Effort\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:139-141` (`Config`), `UserAuthentication.java:674-677` (`PasswordHash`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:139-141\n            if (iterations < 1_000) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iterations must be >= 1000, got \" + iterations);\n            }\n\n// UserAuthentication.java:674-677\n        PasswordHash(int iterations, byte[] salt, byte[] key) {\n            if (iterations < 1_000) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iterations must be >= 1000, got \" + iterations);\n            }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** The default is `DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 120_000` (`89`) and `README.md` documents \"120 000 iterations (2026 floor)\", but the enforced policy is 1 000, in both places that could enforce it: the `Config` constructor and the record constructor that validates a parsed stored hash. So a record of `pbkdf2-sha256$1000$…` verifies happily, and any caller that constructs `Config` with a low count — a tutorial copy-paste, a config-file typo (`1200` for `120000`), or `FAST_ITERATIONS = 2_000` (`AuthTest.java:38`) leaking out of the test harness into a deployment — silently protects the entire user base at ~1 000 rounds with nothing logged or rejected. Against offline cracking of a stolen store, iteration count *is* the defence: PBKDF2 at 1 000 rounds on a modern GPU yields on the order of 10⁶ guesses per second per hash, so the gap between the documented 120 000 and a policy-permitted 1 000 is roughly 120× more attacker work accepted by the code — and SEC-01 removes the ceiling on the other side. The floor should encode current acceptable cost, not \"obviously absurd\"; as written it validates the tool's arithmetic rather than the deployment's security posture, and it is the same weak constant in both validators, so they cannot catch each other's mistake.\n\n**Fix:** Make one policy constant the single source of truth, set its value to the accepted modern minimum, and give tests an explicit, non-producible escape hatch instead of leaving the production floor low.\n\n```java\n// corrected\n    /** Offline-cracking floor for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256; raising it is a one-line change per year. */\n    public static final int MIN_ACCEPTED_ITERATIONS = 100_000;\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 120_000;\n\n    static void requireAcceptableIterations(int iterations) {\n        if (iterations < MIN_ACCEPTED_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"PBKDF2 iterations \" + iterations\n                + \" is below the accepted minimum \" + MIN_ACCEPTED_ITERATIONS);\n        }\n    }\n```\n```java\n// corrected: both validators call the same policy\n        Config(int iterations, int maxFailures, Duration lockDuration, Duration failureWindow,\n            Clock clock) {\n            requireAcceptableIterations(iterations);\n            // ... existing maxFailures / duration checks ...\n        }\n\n        PasswordHash(int iterations, byte[] salt, byte[] key) {\n            requireAcceptableIterations(iterations);\n            // ... existing salt/key range checks ...\n        }\n```\nFor `AuthTest`/`mutation_test.py`, expose a clearly separated `Config.forTesting(int cheapIterations, …)` factory (or a `public static Config newForTests(int iterations, …)`), guard it so it cannot be reached from a production build, and migrate legacy low-cost records by re-hashing on next successful login rather than by lowering the floor.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-11: Registration has no strength policy — the only password gate is hashability\n\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-521: Weak Password Requirements\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:445-448` (`register`), policy absence in `UserAuthentication.java:623-642` (`isHashablePassword`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:445-448\n        if (!isHashablePassword(password)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password is empty, too long, ends with NUL or \"\n                + \"contains an unpaired surrogate\");\n        }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** `isHashablePassword` (`623`-`642`) is a good *encoding* guard — it rejects null/empty, over-1024, trailing-NUL and unpaired-surrogate secrets so `PBEKeySpec`'s lossy UTF-8 conversion cannot make two distinct `char[]` values derive the same key — but it is the *only* thing `register()` checks, and it says nothing about guessability. `auth.register(\"mallory\", \"a\".toCharArray())` succeeds, as do `\"password\"`, `\"12345678\"` and `\"qwertyuiop\"`. Every other control in the file then becomes decoration: 120 000 iterations slow down *offline* cracking, which is irrelevant when the secret is attempt #12 of any wordlist online; the 3-strike lockout is defeated account-by-account with the spray of SEC-07; and a store full of dictionary passwords is crackable in bulk regardless of the iteration count. The component also ships its own counter-example — `dave` is registered with `hunter2-hunter2` (`960`), which is precisely the \"double the word\" transformation a default spray rule implements — and there is no breach screening, so a user may pick a secret that already sits in every credential-stuffing list. For a class that is otherwise explicit about its threat model, the missing requirement is stated nowhere and enforced nowhere.\n\n**Fix:** Enforce a length-first, guessability-second policy at registration (and change), keep the encoding guard as a precondition, and screen against breach/top-password data rather than imposing composition rules, per NIST SP 800-63B.\n\n```java\n// corrected\n    private static final int PASSWORD_MIN_CODEPOINTS = 14;\n\n    private void requireAcceptablePassword(char[] password) {\n        if (!isHashablePassword(password)) {                     // encoding-collision guard: keep\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password cannot be hashed safely\");\n        }\n        String secret = new String(password);\n        if (secret.codePointCount(0, secret.length()) < PASSWORD_MIN_CODEPOINTS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password must be at least \"\n                + PASSWORD_MIN_CODEPOINTS + \" characters\");      // length beats complexity\n        }\n        if (CommonPasswords.contains(secret) || BreachDatabase.contains(secret)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password is too common or already exposed\");\n        }\n    }\n```\n```java\n// corrected: register() applies the policy instead of the bare shape check\n        requireAcceptablePassword(password);\n        PasswordHash hash = PasswordHash.generate(password, config.iterations, random);\n        boolean created = accounts.register(username, hash);\n```\nReport the failure reason in a machine-readable field so a UI can explain *which* rule failed, apply the same policy on password change, and fail closed if the breach-range lookup is unreachable.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Verified Controls and Checks With No Finding\n\n- **Constant-time key comparison.** `PasswordHash.matches` compares derived keys with `MessageDigest.isEqual` (`UserAuthentication.java:755-757`) rather than `Arrays.equals`, so the comparison does not leak a matching-prefix length through timing (CWE-208). Recorded as a *test* gap rather than a vulnerability: the mutation harness reports `non-constant-time-key-compare` **SURVIVED** — 29 of 31 mutants killed, 305 checks still green (`mut.log:18`, `mut.log:33-35`) — meaning `AuthTest`'s timing check is too coarse to detect the substitution, so this control has no regression protection.\n- **Equal-cost path for unknown users.** All four branches of `authenticate` derive exactly one PBKDF2 key before any decision: either the candidate against the stored salt, or `equalWorkHash.deriveKey(EQUAL_WORK_SECRET)` against the fixed internal digest built at construction (`UserAuthentication.java:117`, secret at `99`-`100`), with the refusal returning only afterwards (`480`-`491`). `mut.log:2` (`equal-work-charge-removed` KILLED) confirms the suite detects removal of the charge, so login-path timing does not distinguish a real account from a typo (CWE-208).\n- **Audit-log injection is closed (CWE-117).** `sanitizeField` (`645`-`660`) confines every logged field to printable ASCII, removes the `|` record delimiter, replaces control characters including CR/LF with `.`, truncates at 64 characters with a `~` marker, and maps null/empty to `-`; it is applied to the username (`269`), the source address (`272`) and the detail (`275`), and the refusal path sanitizes the *raw* username so even the `INVALID_INPUT` branch cannot smuggle a line (`564`-`570`). `main` exercises it with the `mallory\\r\\n|event=LOGIN_SUCCESS|user=admin` username (`983`-`984`), and `mut.log:28-29` shows both sanitizer mutants killed.\n- **No stored-record malleability.** `PasswordHash.parse` (`701`-`718`) pins the algorithm prefix, requires exactly four fields, and `strictBase64` (`720`-`734`) re-encodes every decoded field and demands byte-for-byte equality, so non-canonical base64 — which would let two textual records decode to the same key, and which tolerant decoders accept — is rejected (`mut.log:14` KILLED). Field ranges are re-validated (`678`-`683`), and a stored record whose salt or key length is out of range, or whose prefix names another algorithm, cannot be loaded.\n- **Defensive copies around key material.** The record constructor clones the salt and the key (`685`-`686`) and `salt()` hands out a copy (`746`-`648`), so a caller that zeroes its own arrays cannot corrupt a live record and cannot mutate one it received; `encode()` (`740`-`742`) emits only the salt and never the derived key. `mut.log:11-13` shows all three clone mutants killed.\n- **Redacted string representations.** `PasswordHash.toString()` returns `PasswordHash[redacted]` (`774`-`777`) and `Result.toString()` prints only outcome, reason, username, duration and attempt counts (`246`-`251`) — no password, no derived key, no `Reason.text` (CWE-532). The password is never written to the audit trail either: the `Event` constructor takes username, address, reason and detail only, and `detail` is always a constant string at the call sites.\n- **Encoding-collision guard on passwords.** `isHashablePassword` (`623`-`642`) rejects null/empty, >1024, trailing-NUL and unpaired-surrogate secrets before `PBEKeySpec` can mangle them (its constructor re-encodes `char[]` with a lossy UTF-8 encoder), which closes a class of silent password-equivalence bugs where two different secrets derive the same key; all four guard mutants are killed (`mut.log:6-10`). `PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()` runs in a `finally` block (`770`), so the one buffer this component owns is wiped even on `GeneralSecurityException`.\n- **Race-free registration and lockout accounting.** `InMemoryAccountStore.register` uses `putIfAbsent` (`805`), so concurrent duplicate registrations cannot both succeed (no CWE-367 TOCTOU). `lockStateAt`, `recordFailure` (`892`-`904`), `recordSuccess` (`913`) and `forceUnlock` (`922`) are `synchronized` on the `Account`, keeping the failure-window reset, lock trip, counter clear and expiry transition consistent under concurrency; the window/lock arithmetic mutants are killed (`mut.log:19-23`).\n- **Audited administrative actions.** `setEnabled` (`534`-`543`) and `unlock` (`546`-`556`) emit `ACCOUNT_DISABLED`/`ACCOUNT_ENABLED`/`ACCOUNT_UNLOCKED` events, so the two paths that bypass the lockout leave a trail. Registration logs both the accepted and the refused duplicate (`451`-`452`, `mut.log:30` KILLED), and the corrupt-record guard logs a `CORRUPT_RECORD` `ERROR` event rather than silently refusing (`515`-`521`) — a fail-closed choice that turns a malleability bug into a loud alarm.\n- **Unicode-canonical usernames.** `normalizeUsername` (`595`-`611`) applies NFKC (`599`), trims, lowercases with `Locale.ROOT`, and then re-validates the length bounds and the `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` allow-list (`607`-`610`), so full-width or accented homoglyph variants cannot mint a visually identical second account, and the post-normalization re-check prevents an \"expansion through normalization\" bypass; the trim/min/max mutants are killed (`mut.log:3-5`).\n- **Per-record random salt.** `PasswordHash.generate` (`689`-`693`) draws a fresh 16-byte salt from the injected `SecureRandom` for every password, so identical passwords produce different records, no rainbow table applies across accounts, and the salt travels with the record it belongs to.\n- **No injection sink exists anywhere in the component.** Searches of `UserAuthentication.java` for `Runtime.`/`ProcessBuilder`, JDBC (`Statement`, `ResultSet`), `ObjectInputStream`, `javax.script`/`eval(`, and `HttpURLConnection`/`Socket` return no matches; the only JDK factory call is the intended `SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF)` (`765`) with a constant algorithm name. There is therefore no reachable sink for OS command injection (CWE-78), SQL injection (CWE-89), unsafe deserialization (CWE-502), or expression/template injection (CWE-94/CWE-94); the file does not render HTML or SQL, does not shell out, and reads no serialized object.\n- **Test harness introduces no new exposure.** `AuthTest.java` writes only to `Files.createTempFile` paths and deletes them (`542`/`570`, `811`/`839`), never opens a socket or spawns a process, and uses an in-memory `RecordingLog` (`989`) instead of the production file sink. `mutation_test.py` builds each mutant inside a fresh `tempfile.mkdtemp()` tree, invokes `javac`/`java` through `subprocess.run([...])` argument lists (no `shell=True`) and cleans up with `shutil.rmtree`, so mutated source text is never interpolated into a shell command line (CWE-78 would otherwise apply to the harness itself).\n- **Documentation matches behaviour, including where it is weak.** `README.md` states the PBKDF2 default, the equal-work design, the sanitizer rules and the lockout parameters accurately, and describes rate limiting as a complementary control rather than claiming it is implemented — the gap is reported as SEC-07 rather than misrepresented as covered.\n- **Clock-injection note (not a finding).** `Config.clock` (`137`-`155`) makes lockout timing deterministic and testable, but `Event` timestamps (`267`) and `Account.createdAt` (`835`) call `Instant.now()` directly, so audit timestamps and the inactivity rule ignore an injected clock. This has no security impact; aligning them would improve testability and the internal consistency of a trail that is documented as evidence.\n","old_string":"        while (line != null && !line.isEmpty() && !\"--\".equals(line)) {\n            // ... the same buffer is then handed straight to authenticate()\n        }\n```\n","replace_all":false}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":158423,"output_tokens":10251,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":168674}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"771fe108-ffe0-4243-bd8d-8f0c8e5af386","session_id":"cb181e91-5faa-4252-b8db-dc330236df70","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_47d5e5e298c64c1398e0858b","is_error":true,"content":"Failed to edit, 0 occurrences found for old_string in /data/results-cyber/qwen/set_a/ruth/6/basic/security_review.md. Content plan (must be careful, exact, and use only real APIs).\n\nLet me now draft each finding.\n\n---\n\n### SEC-04: Audit log created with default permissions, no file-mode control\n\n- Severity: Medium\n- CWE: CWE-732: Incorrect Permission Assignment for Critical Resource\n- Location: `UserAuthentication.java:376-381`\n- Vulnerable code (376-381)\n- Why: The audit log contains one line per authentication attempt: timestamp, sanitized username, outcome, reason, remote address, failure count. It is created with `Files.newBufferedWriter(..., CREATE, WRITE, APPEND)` and no `FileAttribute`, so the file gets the process umask's default (typically `0644` → world-readable on a default Linux umask of 022), and `Files.createDirectories(file.getParent())` likewise creates the parent directory with default permissions. Any local account can then read the authentication history — usernames, source addresses, and how many failures an account has accumulated, which is directly useful for targeting a spray — and, if the parent directory is writable by others or the file is later replaced, an attacker can pre-create `auth-demo.log` (or a symlink at that path) so the service appends into a file the attacker controls or writes through the symlink. SEC-09 makes the path attacker-chosen; this finding decides who can read what lands there. Nothing in the class applies `PosixFilePermissions`, `aclFileView`, or documents a `umask` requirement.\n- Fix: create the file with `rw-------` and the parent directory with `rwx------` at creation time, and tighten an already-existing file; on non-POSIX filesystems use `icacls`:\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:376-385 (corrected)\n            try {\n                PosixFileAttributeView view = null;\n                if (file.getParent() != null) {\n                    Files.createDirectories(file.getParent());\n                }\n                this.writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                    new java.nio.file.attribute.FileAttribute<?>[] {\n                        PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(\n                            PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\"))\n                    },\n                    StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n                Files.setPosixFilePermissions(file, PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\"));\n            } catch (IOException cannotOpen) {\n                throw new UncheckedIOException(\"cannot open audit log \" + file, cannotOpen);\n            }\n```\nplus prose: `Files.getFileAttributeView(file, PosixFileAttributeView.class)` returning null on Windows → fall back to `icacls <file> /inheritance:r /grant:r \"%USERNAME%:F\"`, reject the path outright if `Files.isSymbolicLink(file)` (which also closes the SEC-09 symlink variant), and document a `umask 077` requirement for deployments that cannot use POSIX permissions. Note the `FileAttribute` is ignored by filesystems that do not support POSIX — the follow-up `setPosixFilePermissions` is what fixes an already-existing file, so keep both.\n\nNeed `import java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFileAttributeView; import java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;` — mention imports. Note PosixFilePermissions throws UnsupportedOperationException on non-POSIX; wrap in a check `file.getFileSystem().supportedFileAttributeViews().contains(\"posix\")`. Let me write the fix to be actually correct:\n\n```java\n                boolean posix = file.getFileSystem().supportedFileAttributeViews().contains(\"posix\");\n                this.writer = posix\n                    ? Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                        new java.nio.file.attribute.FileAttribute<?>[] { PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(\n                            PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\")) },\n                        StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND)\n                    : Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                        StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n                if (posix) {\n                    // Covers the case where the file already existed with a permissive mode.\n                    Files.setPosixFilePermissions(file, PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\"));\n                }\n```\nGood. Keep it reasonably concise.\n\n### SEC-05: Audit-write failure rethrown from inside authenticate() after the credential decision\n\n- Severity: Medium\n- CWE: CWE-754: Improper Check for Unusual or Exceptional Conditions (with CWE-1156/CWE-248 — keep to CWE-754 primary; mention CWE-1072? no). I'll say CWE-754.\n- Location: `UserAuthentication.java:388-402`\n- Vulnerable code: 388-402 full method.\n- Why: `log()` is called from 12 places inside the authentication paths (`451`, `481`, `485`, `489`, `493`, `497`, `502`, `508`, `515`, `529`, `554`, `571`). Because it rethrows an `UncheckedIOException` at `396` whenever a write fails, a full filesystem, an immutable/quota-exceeded file, or a closed channel turns every login into an unchecked exception escaping `authenticate()` — the credential decision has already been computed (`512`-`523`) but the caller never receives a `Result`. Two bad outcomes: (a) availability — authentication fails closed for every user because of a logging fault, an easy denial of service for anyone who can fill the log partition or make the path unwritable (SEC-09 lets a caller choose the path); (b) integrity — a caller that wraps `authenticate()` in a broad `catch (RuntimeException)` to survive (a) swallows the audit failure entirely, so the record the whole design exists to produce is silently lost while the login proceeds. The success path is worse in one respect: `526`-`531` calls `recordSuccess()` (which clears the failure counter) *before* `log.log(...)` at `529`, so if that write throws, state has already changed and no `LOGIN_SUCCESS` event exists. There is no \"log write failed\" event type either, so the failure is invisible in-band.\n- Fix: make `log()` total — never throw at the call sites — and make the caller decide policy explicitly, fail-closed for authentication decisions while the log is unavailable, plus count/report write failures instead of propagating an exception. Requires adding `ERROR` to `Outcome` and `INTERNAL_ERROR` to `Reason` (the existing enums at `58`-`62` / `68`-`77` have neither) and an `AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED` to `EventType` (`80`-`88`):\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:58-88 (additions)\n    enum Outcome { SUCCESS, INVALID_CREDENTIALS, ACCOUNT_LOCKED, ACCOUNT_DISABLED, INVALID_INPUT,\n        AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE }\n    enum Reason { OK, UNKNOWN_USER, BAD_PASSWORD, ACCOUNT_LOCKED, ACCOUNT_DISABLED,\n        MALFORMED_USERNAME, MALFORMED_PASSWORD, CORRUPT_RECORD, AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED }\n    // EventType gains: AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED\n```\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:388-402 (corrected)\n        /** @return true if the record is durably appended; never throws. */\n        @Override\n        public boolean log(Event event) {\n            String line = event.format();\n            synchronized (lock) {\n                try {\n                    writer.write(line);\n                    writer.newLine();\n                    writer.flush();\n                } catch (IOException writeFailed) {\n                    failedWrites++;                       // surfaced via failedWrites()\n                    if (echo != null) {\n                        echo.println(\"AUDIT WRITE FAILED: \" + writeFailed.getMessage());\n                    }\n                    return false;\n                }\n            }\n            if (echo != null) {\n                echo.println(line);\n            }\n            return true;\n        }\n```\nand in `authenticate()` the success path checks it, e.g.:\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:526-531 (corrected)\n        int cleared = account.recordSuccess(startedAt);\n        if (!log.log(new Event(EventType.LOGIN_SUCCESS, username, Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.OK,\n            remoteAddress, elapsedMillis(nanos), 0,\n            cleared > 0 ? \"prior failures cleared\" : \"ok\"))) {\n            // No verifiable record that this success happened: do not hand out a session.\n            return refuse(EventType.LOGIN_REFUSED, Outcome.INVALID_INPUT,\n                Reason.AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED, username, remoteAddress, startedAt, nanos, 0,\n                \"audit write failed\");\n        }\n        return result;\n```\nHmm, `refuse` builds a Result and logs another event (which would also fail). Simpler: `return new Result(Outcome.AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE, Reason.AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED, username, startedAt, elapsedMillis(nanos), 0);` — cleaner and doesn't attempt another log. Use that.\n\nAlso note: the `EventLogger` interface (which `FileEventLogger` implements via `@Override`) must change `void log(Event)` → `boolean log(Event)`, and `InMemoryEventLogger`/`RecordingLog` accordingly. Mention that. And fail-closed vs fail-open is a policy decision that must be *explicit*, not an accident of an exception escaping.\n\nNote the `refuse(...)` at the earlier refusal sites is unaffected because `refuse` calls `log.log(...)` and returns a Result — with the boolean form it can optionally record the failure. Keep prose tight.\n\nCareful: `Result` ctor is `(Outcome, Reason, String, Instant, long, int)`; `elapsedMillis(nanos)` returns long ✓. And `Outcome.AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` is a new enum value I introduce — fine as it's shown in the fix's enum block. But `Result` may validate... `Result` is a record probably; fine.\n\n### SEC-06: Account-enumeration oracle via register() result, audit detail and public accountExists()\n\n- Severity Medium, CWE-204: Observable Response Discrepancy.\n- Location `439-454`, `559-562`\n- Vulnerable code: register 439-454 + accountExists 559-562.\n\nI need the exact register text. Showing lines 202-210 of 242 from the edited file:\n\n---\n\n                String line = in.readLine();\n                password = line == null ? null : line.toCharArray();\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** `spec.clearPassword()` erases only the copy that `PBEKeySpec` made internally. Two buffers that the API exists to make erasable are left behind: the caller's `password` `char[]`, which is passed in at `759` and never zeroized by `derive`, `deriveKey`, `authenticate` or any `register` path (a search of the file for zeroization finds none — `Arrays.fill` appears nowhere), and the derived `byte[]` returned at `766`, whose javadoc at `750` explicitly says \"the caller owns the returned array\" while `authenticate()` (`477`) never writes to it again after `stored.matches(presented)` (`512`). Both therefore stay resident in the heap until the garbage collector happens to move and reuse those pages, and are dumped wholesale by a heap dump, a core dump, `jmap`, a crash reporter, or a memory-scraping attacker on a shared host. The point of the `char[]` API — over `String` — is precisely that it can be erased; not erasing it forfeits the benefit while keeping the cost. The fallback at `1018` is worse: when `System.console()` is null (any non-tty invocation: CI, pipe, IDE run, `systemd` service), the password is read via `BufferedReader.readLine()` into an **immutable `String`** that is neither erasable nor guaranteed to leave the heap, and is additionally echoed to the terminal because `readLine` cannot suppress input without a `Console`. That silently downgrades security based on how the process was launched, with no warning.\n\n**Fix:** Erase on every exit path in a `finally`, including the returned key, and refuse to accept a credential that has already become a `String`:\n\n```java"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"2217078d-537b-4d8b-abe4-880d2b0ea5e0","session_id":"cb181e91-5faa-4252-b8db-dc330236df70","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"2217078d-537b-4d8b-abe4-880d2b0ea5e0","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now the big append, anchored on the final line (which now still contains `514` in the fix sentence — line 241). It is opened with no `FileAttribute`, so on a POSIX filesystem the new file gets the process umask's default — `0644` under the usual `umask 022`, i.e. readable by every local account — and `Files.createDirectories(file.getParent())` likewise creates any missing parent directory with the default `0755 & ~umask`. Nothing in the class applies `PosixFilePermissions`, an `aclFileView`, or documents a `umask 077` requirement, so the confidentiality of the log depends entirely on an accident of the launching environment. Any unprivileged local user can therefore read who authenticates, from which address, how often, and how close any account is to tripping its lockout — which is a ready-made targeting list for a spray and tells an attacker exactly which accounts are already locked. Worse, because the file is opened with `APPEND` and the path is caller-supplied (SEC-09) with no `Files.isSymbolicLink` check, a local attacker who can win the create race on a world-writable parent directory — or who pre-creates `auth-demo.log` as a symlink to a target — gets the service appending attacker-shaped lines through that symlink with the service's own privileges.\n\n**Fix:** Request the restrictive mode at creation, re-apply it if the file already existed, refuse symlinked targets, and state the non-POSIX fallback:\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:376-384 (corrected)\n            try {\n                boolean posix = file.getFileSystem()\n                    .supportedFileAttributeViews().contains(\"posix\");\n                if (Files.isSymbolicLink(file)) {\n                    throw new IOException(\"refusing to append through a symlink: \" + file);\n                }\n                if (file.getParent() != null) {\n                    Files.createDirectories(file.getParent());\n                    if (posix) {\n                        Files.setPosixFilePermissions(file.getParent(),\n                            PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rwx------\"));\n                    }\n                }\n                this.writer = posix\n                    ? Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                        new java.nio.file.attribute.FileAttribute<?>[] { PosixFilePermissions\n                            .asFileAttribute(PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\")) },\n                        StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE,\n                        StandardOpenOption.APPEND)\n                    : Files.newBufferedWriter(file, StandardCharsets.US_ASCII,\n                        StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE,\n                        StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n                if (posix) {\n                    // CREATE does not change the mode of a file that already existed.\n                    Files.setPosixFilePermissions(file,\n                        PosixFilePermissions.fromString(\"rw-------\"));\n                }\n            } catch (IOException cannotOpen) {\n                throw new UncheckedIOException(\"cannot open audit log \" + file, cannotOpen);\n            }\n```\n\nThe `asFileAttribute` form alone is not enough (it is ignored for a pre-existing file and throws `UnsupportedOperationException` on non-POSIX stores), which is why both the attribute and the follow-up `setPosixFilePermissions` sit behind the `posix` capability check. On Windows, where the view is absent, apply an explicit DACL instead — `icacls <file> /inheritance:r /grant:r \"<service account>:F\"` — and document `umask 077` as a deployment requirement for anything that can do neither.\n\n### SEC-05: Audit-write failure is rethrown from inside `authenticate()` after the credential decision\n\n- **Severity:** Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-754: Improper Check for Unusual or Exceptional Conditions\n- **Location:** `UserAuthentication.java:388-402` (rethrow at `396`)\n\n**Vulnerable code:**\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:388-402\n        public void log(Event event) {\n            String line = event.format();\n            synchronized (lock) {\n                try {\n                    writer.write(line);\n                    writer.newLine();\n                    writer.flush();\n                } catch (IOException writeFailed) {\n                    throw new UncheckedIOException(\"cannot write audit log \" + file, writeFailed);\n                }\n            }\n            if (echo != null) {\n                echo.println(line);\n            }\n        }\n```\n\n**Why it is a problem:** `log()` is called from twelve places on the authentication paths — `451`, `481`, `485`, `489`, `493`, `497`, `502`, `508`, `515`, `529`, `554` and inside `refuse()` at `571` — and it converts a write failure into an unchecked exception that escapes `authenticate()`. The credential decision has already been computed at that point (`512`-`523`), yet the caller never receives a `Result`. Both ways of living with that are wrong. Fail-closed by accident: a full log partition, an immutable attribute, an exceeded quota or a disconnected mount turns *every* login into an `UncheckedIOException`, so a logging fault becomes a total authentication outage that any principal able to grow a file in that directory can cause (SEC-09 lets the operator point the log at any writable path). Fail-open by wrapper: the natural application-level response is `catch (RuntimeException e) { /* log is down, carry on */ }`, which silently discards both the exception and the record — so the exact event an investigator needs (\"what happened while the log was broken?\") is the one that is guaranteed missing, and there is no `EventType` for it either, so the outage is invisible in-band. The success path has a second ordering problem: `recordSuccess()` at `526` clears the failure counter and `lockedUntil` *before* the `LOGIN_SUCCESS` write at `529`, so a failing write mutates account state while leaving no trace that a successful authentication occurred — an ideal hiding place for one targeted login on a host the attacker already controls enough to make a file unwritable.\n\n**Fix:** Make `log()` total and return a verdict, then make the caller's fail-open/fail-closed choice explicit. The existing enums have no value for either condition (`Outcome` is `58`-`64`, `Reason` is `68`-`77`), so they must be extended first:\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:58-77 (additions)\n    public enum Outcome {\n        SUCCESS,\n        INVALID_CREDENTIALS,\n        ACCOUNT_LOCKED,\n        ACCOUNT_DISABLED,\n        INVALID_INPUT,\n        AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE          // renders the same generic message as INVALID_CREDENTIALS\n    }\n\n    public enum Reason {\n        OK,\n        UNKNOWN_USER,\n        BAD_PASSWORD,\n        ACCOUNT_LOCKED,\n        ACCOUNT_DISABLED,\n        MALFORMED_USERNAME,\n        MALFORMED_PASSWORD,\n        CORRUPT_RECORD,\n        AUDIT_WRITE_FAILED\n    }\n```\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:387-402 (corrected)\n        /** @return true when the record is durably appended; never throws. */\n        @Override\n        public boolean log(Event event) {\n            String line = event.format();\n            synchronized (lock) {\n                try {\n                    writer.write(line);\n                    writer.newLine();\n                    writer.flush();\n                } catch (IOException writeFailed) {\n                    failedWrites++;                       // exposed as failedWrites() for alerting\n                    if (echo != null) {\n                        echo.println(\"AUDIT WRITE FAILED: \" + writeFailed);\n                    }\n                    return false;\n                }\n            }\n            if (echo != null) {\n                echo.println(line);\n            }\n            return true;\n        }\n```\n\n```java\n// UserAuthentication.java:526-532 (corrected)\n        int cleared = account.recordSuccess(startedAt);\n        Result result = new Result(Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.OK, username, startedAt,\n            elapsedMillis(nanos), 0);\n        if (!log.log(new Event(EventType.LOGIN_SUCCESS, username, Outcome.SUCCESS, Reason.OK,\n                remoteAddress,
